I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but I’ll just be at my house or in my office, doing ordinary things, and suddenly it feels unreal—like I’m watching myself from across the room instead of really being there. I’m not sure if stress is part of it or not, but I haven’t only noticed it during some big stressful moment.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if these feelings of unreality or watching yourself happen in distinct episodes, or if they feel more like a constant background experience that comes and goes throughout your day?\n\nPatient: It feels more like distinct moments, I think. The symptoms are not constant but occur intermittently, several times a week, and are characterized by derealization (environment feeling unreal) and depersonalization (feeling detached from oneself, as if watching from a distance). These symptoms align with a diagnosis of Depersonalization-Derealization Disorder (F44.2). Other dissociative disorders such as F44.8 (Other specified dissociative disorders) and F44.9 (Dissociative disorder, unspecified) are ruled out as the symptoms are best explained by F44.2 and there is no evidence of more generalized dissociation or identity disturbance.", "is_final": true}
